People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. I don’t want it to sound dramatic, but like, if I look in the mirror before class or before softball, I notice my stomach and my thighs right away. They still look… bigger than they should, to me.\n\nAnd then it kind of decides how I feel about the day, I guess. Like if they look okay, I feel like I’m doing good, and if not, it feels like I’m messing everything up. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make it sound like I’m terrified or anything, but I do get really nervous about it.\n\nLike if I eat fried food from the break room, or if I have a big plate at church, I start thinking it’s going to show on me right away. Then I keep going over it in my head and I want to check the scale, just to make sure I didn’t mess up. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I mean, some, yeah. I don’t know if I’d call it a lot, exactly.\n\nMy work pants are definitely looser, and people have said I look too thin, but… when I see the number go down, it kind of feels like I’m finally getting somewhere. So it’s hard for me to understand why everyone wants me to put it back on, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a huge amount in an hour or anything. I mean, sometimes I feel like I ate too much, but I don’t know if it would count as a “large amount.”\n\nSorry, I’m not trying to be difficult—I just… I guess I’m not sure what would count for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t make myself throw up after eating.\n\nI mean, I feel really guilty sometimes, but I don’t do that. I don’t want it to sound dramatic, but like, if I look in the mirror before class or before softball, I notice my stomach and my thighs right away. They still look… bigger than they should, to me.\n\nAnd then it kind of decides how I feel about the day, I guess. Like if they look okay, I feel like I’m doing good, and if not, it feels like I’m messing everything up. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make it sound like I’m terrified or anything, but I do get really nervous about it.\n\nLike if I eat fried food from the break room, or if I have a big plate at church, I start thinking it’s going to show on me right away. Then I keep going over it in my head and I want to check the scale, just to make sure I didn’t mess up. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I mean, some, yeah. I don’t know if I’d call it a lot, exactly.\n\nMy work pants are definitely looser, and people have said I look too thin, but… when I see the number go down, it kind of feels like I’m finally getting somewhere. So it’s hard for me to understand why everyone wants me to put it back on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is actually different from how others see it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, people tell me I look thin or that I shouldn’t worry, and I try to believe them, but when I look in the mirror, especially before class or softball, my stomach and my thighs still look… too big to me.\n\nIt probably sounds silly, but it can kind of set the whole day. Like if I think they look okay, I feel like I’m doing good, and if they don’t, it feels like I’m failing. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a huge amount in an hour or anything. I mean, sometimes I feel like I ate too much, but I don’t know if it would count as a “large amount.”\n\nSorry, I’m not trying to be difficult—I just… I guess I’m not sure what would count for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t make myself throw up after eating.\n\nI mean, I feel really guilty sometimes, but I don’t do that. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to try to burn off calories after eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it excessive… I mean, I do try to be active, but after eating specifically to burn it off? I’m not sure.\n\nSometimes I feel like I should move around or do something, but I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. What would count as excessive?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t take anything like that to lose weight.\n\nI’m sorry, I’m not totally sure what counts as a diuretic, but I don’t use laxatives or water pills for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating and ended up eating much more than you intended?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like, out of control.\n\nSometimes I’ll eat more than I planned and then feel bad about it, but I wouldn’t say it’s a huge amount or like I couldn’t stop. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I don’t want it to sound dramatic, but like, if I look in the mirror before class or before softball, I notice my stomach and my thighs right away. They still look… bigger than they should, to me.\n\nAnd then it kind of decides how I feel about the day, I guess. Like if they look okay, I feel like I’m doing good, and if not, it feels like I’m messing everything up. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make it sound like I’m terrified or anything, but I do get really nervous about it.\n\nLike if I eat fried food from the break room, or if I have a big plate at church, I start thinking it’s going to show on me right away. Then I keep going over it in my head and I want to check the scale, just to make sure I didn’t mess up. I’m not really sure… I don’t think so? Like, if I eat something that feels like more than I’m used to, I can get kind of aware of it, but I don’t know if I’d call it extremely full.

I don’t think I’m trying to make it a big thing, but most days I’ll just have coffee before I go to the warehouse, and then maybe a yogurt or a few bites of something after GED class.\n\nIt’s just… sitting down for a full meal feels like it messes with everything I’m trying to keep in line, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your weight or body shape when you decide what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I guess I do. I don’t want it to sound dramatic, but like, if I look in the mirror before class or before softball, I notice my stomach and my thighs right away. They still look… bigger than they should, to me.\n\nAnd then it kind of decides how I feel about the day, I guess. Like if they look okay, I feel like I’m doing good, and if not, it feels like I’m messing everything up. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make it sound like I’m terrified or anything, but I do get really nervous about it.\n\nLike if I eat fried food from the break room, or if I have a big plate at church, I start thinking it’s going to show on me right away. Then I keep going over it in my head and I want to check the scale, just to make sure I didn’t mess up. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I mean, some, yeah. I don’t know if I’d call it a lot, exactly.\n\nMy work pants are definitely looser, and people have said I look too thin, but… when I see the number go down, it kind of feels like I’m finally getting somewhere. So it’s hard for me to understand why everyone wants me to put it back on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is actually different from how others see it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, people tell me I look thin or that I shouldn’t worry, and I try to believe them, but when I look in the mirror, especially before class or softball, my stomach and my thighs still look… too big to me.\n\nIt probably sounds silly, but it can kind of set the whole day. Like if I think they look okay, I feel like I’m doing good, and if they don’t, it feels like I’m failing. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a huge amount in an hour or anything. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to try to burn off calories after eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it excessive… I mean, I do try to be active, but after eating specifically to burn it off? I mean, I don’t take anything like that to lose weight.\n\nI’m sorry, I’m not totally sure what counts as a diuretic, but I don’t use laxatives or water pills for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating and ended up eating much more than you intended?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like, out of control.\n\nSometimes I’ll eat more than I planned and then feel bad about it, but I wouldn’t say it’s a huge amount or like I couldn’t stop. It’s just sitting down for a real meal feels like it messes up what I’m trying to keep in line, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight affect your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still getting to work and my GED class, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not functioning.\n\nBut I have been saying no when people ask me to eat after church, and sometimes I don’t finish my GED homework because I get too caught up in keeping my eating and my schedule under control. I know that probably sounds silly… but it does take up a lot of my day.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten a normal amount of food, but then still feel extremely full or uncomfortable afterwards?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I don’t think so? I don’t think I’m trying to make it a big thing, but most days I’ll just have coffee before I go to the warehouse, and then maybe a yogurt or a few bites of something after GED class.\n\nIt’s just… sitting down for a full meal feels like it messes with everything I’m trying to keep in line, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your weight or body shape when you decide what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I guess I do. I don’t want it to sound dramatic, but like, if I look in the mirror before class or before softball, I notice my stomach and my thighs right away. They still look… bigger than they should, to me.\n\nAnd then it kind of decides how I feel about the day, I guess. Like if they look okay, I feel like I’m doing good, and if not, it feels like I’m messing everything up. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make it sound like I’m terrified or anything, but I do get really nervous about it.\n\nLike if I eat fried food from the break room, or if I have a big plate at church, I start thinking it’s going to show on me right away. Then I keep going over it in my head and I want to check the scale, just to make sure I didn’t mess up. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I mean, some, yeah. I don’t know if I’d call it a lot, exactly.\n\nMy work pants are definitely looser, and people have said I look too thin, but… when I see the number go down, it kind of feels like I’m finally getting somewhere. So it’s hard for me to understand why everyone wants me to put it back on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is actually different from how others see it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, people tell me I look thin or that I shouldn’t worry, and I try to believe them, but when I look in the mirror, especially before class or softball, my stomach and my thighs still look… too big to me.\n\nIt probably sounds silly, but it can kind of set the whole day. Like if I think they look okay, I feel like I’m doing good, and if they don’t, it feels like I’m failing. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a huge amount in an hour or anything. I mean, sometimes I feel like I ate too much, but I don’t know if it would count as a “large amount.”\n\nSorry, I’m not trying to be difficult—I just… I guess I’m not sure what would count for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t make myself throw up after eating.\n\nI mean, I feel really guilty sometimes, but I don’t do that. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to try to burn off calories after eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it excessive… I mean, I do try to be active, but after eating specifically to burn it off? I’m not sure.\n\nSometimes I feel like I should move around or do something, but I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. What would count as excessive?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t take anything like that to lose weight.\n\nI’m sorry, I’m not totally sure what counts as a diuretic, but I don’t use laxatives or water pills for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating and ended up eating much more than you intended?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like, out of control.\n\nSometimes I’ll eat more than I planned and then feel bad about it, but I wouldn’t say it’s a huge amount or like I couldn’t stop. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you often go a long time without eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, sometimes. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic, but most days I’ll just have coffee before I go to the warehouse, and then maybe later, after GED class, I’ll have a yogurt or just a few bites of something.\n\nThe clinic’s kind of been on me about how little I’m eating, so… I know it probably sounds not great. It’s just sitting down for a real meal feels like it messes up what I’m trying to keep in line, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight affect your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still getting to work and my GED class, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not functioning.\n\nBut I have been saying no when people ask me to eat after church, and sometimes I don’t finish my GED homework because I get too caught up in keeping my eating and my schedule under control. I know that probably sounds silly… but it does take up a lot of my day.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten a normal amount of food, but then still feel extremely full or uncomfortable afterwards?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I don’t think so? Like, if I eat something that feels like more than I’m used to, I can get kind of aware of it, but I don’t know if I’d call it extremely full.\n\nMaybe uncomfortable sometimes, but I thought that was just because I’m not used to eating much at once. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a significant amount of weight loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it’s been enough that people have noticed. My work pants are pretty loose now, and a couple people have said I look too thin.\n\nBut I don’t know… when I see the number going down, part of me feels like I’m finally doing something right, like I’m getting somewhere. So it’s hard for me to understand why everyone wants me to put it back on. Is that… the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to try to burn off calories after eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it excessive… I mean, I do try to be active, but after eating specifically to burn it off? What would count as excessive?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t take anything like that to lose weight.\n\nI’m sorry, I’m not totally sure what counts as a diuretic, but I don’t use laxatives or water pills for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating and ended up eating much more than you intended?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like, out of control.\n\nSometimes I’ll eat more than I planned and then feel bad about it, but I wouldn’t say it’s a huge amount or like I couldn’t stop. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you often go a long time without eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, sometimes. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic, but most days I’ll just have coffee before I go to the warehouse, and then maybe later, after GED class, I’ll have a yogurt or just a few bites of something.\n\nThe clinic’s kind of been on me about how little I’m eating, so… I know it probably sounds not great. It’s just sitting down for a real meal feels like it messes up what I’m trying to keep in line, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight affect your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still getting to work and my GED class, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not functioning.\n\nBut I have been saying no when people ask me to eat after church, and sometimes I don’t finish my GED homework because I get too caught up in keeping my eating and my schedule under control. I know that probably sounds silly… but it does take up a lot of my day.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten a normal amount of food, but then still feel extremely full or uncomfortable afterwards?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I don’t think so? Like, if I eat something that feels like more than I’m used to, I can get kind of aware of it, but I don’t know if I’d call it extremely full.\n\nMaybe uncomfortable sometimes, but I thought that was just because I’m not used to eating much at once. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a significant amount of weight loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it’s been enough that people have noticed. My work pants are pretty loose now, and a couple people have said I look too thin.\n\nBut I don’t know… when I see the number going down, part of me feels like I’m finally doing something right, like I’m getting somewhere. So it’s hard for me to understand why everyone wants me to put it back on. Is that… the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you didn't worry about how your body looks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I probably would. I don’t want to make it sound like it’s only about looks, but when I check the mirror before GED class or softball, my stomach and thighs still look… bigger than they should to me.\n\nAnd then it kind of sets the tone for the whole day, like I’m either doing good or I’m failing. So if I didn’t have that in my head, I think eating more would feel less scary. I know that probably sounds silly… but it does take up a lot of my day.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten a normal amount of food, but then still feel extremely full or uncomfortable afterwards?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I don’t think so? Like, if I eat something that feels like more than I’m used to, I can get kind of aware of it, but I don’t know if I’d call it extremely full.\n\nMaybe uncomfortable sometimes, but I thought that was just because I’m not used to eating much at once. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a significant amount of weight loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it’s been enough that people have noticed. My work pants are pretty loose now, and a couple people have said I look too thin.\n\nBut I don’t know… when I see the number going down, part of me feels like I’m finally doing something right, like I’m getting somewhere. So it’s hard for me to understand why everyone wants me to put it back on. Is that… the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you didn't worry about how your body looks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I probably would. I don’t want to make it sound like it’s only about looks, but when I check the mirror before GED class or softball, my stomach and thighs still look… bigger than they should to me.\n\nAnd then it kind of sets the tone for the whole day, like I’m either doing good or I’m failing. So if I didn’t have that in my head, I think eating more would feel less scary. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would see yourself as overweight even if other people told you that you are underweight?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, probably. I mean, I know people say I’m too thin, and I’m trying to hear that, but when I look in the mirror before class or softball, I still see my stomach and thighs as bigger than they should be.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to argue with everyone, it just feels real to me in the moment. And then it kind of decides whether I feel like I’m doing okay or like I failed for the whole day. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would change if you had a more accurate perception of your body weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably would. I don’t want to overstate it, but a lot of it starts when I look in the mirror, like before class or softball, and my stomach and thighs still look bigger than they should to me.\n\nIf I could see that more clearly, I think eating wouldn’t feel so much like I’m doing something wrong. Right now it kind of makes the whole day feel like I’m either doing good or I’m failing… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight interfere with your daily activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to let it, but it does get in the way a lot. I mean, sometimes I feel like I ate too much, but I don’t know if it would count as a “large amount.”\n\nSorry, I’m not trying to be difficult—I just… I guess I’m not sure what would count for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t make myself throw up after eating.\n\nI mean, I feel really guilty sometimes, but I don’t do that. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to try to burn off calories after eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it excessive… I mean, I do try to be active, but after eating specifically to burn it off? I’m not sure.\n\nSometimes I feel like I should move around or do something, but I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. What would count as excessive?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t take anything like that to lose weight.\n\nI’m sorry, I’m not totally sure what counts as a diuretic, but I don’t use laxatives or water pills for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating and ended up eating much more than you intended?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like, out of control.\n\nSometimes I’ll eat more than I planned and then feel bad about it, but I wouldn’t say it’s a huge amount or like I couldn’t stop. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you often go a long time without eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, sometimes. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic, but most days I’ll just have coffee before I go to the warehouse, and then maybe later, after GED class, I’ll have a yogurt or just a few bites of something.\n\nThe clinic’s kind of been on me about how little I’m eating, so… I know it probably sounds not great. It’s just sitting down for a real meal feels like it messes up what I’m trying to keep in line, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight affect your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still getting to work and my GED class, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not functioning.\n\nBut I have been saying no when people ask me to eat after church, and sometimes I don’t finish my GED homework because I get too caught up in keeping my eating and my schedule under control. I know that probably sounds silly… but it does take up a lot of my day.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten a normal amount of food, but then still feel extremely full or uncomfortable afterwards?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I don’t think so? Like, if I eat something that feels like more than I’m used to, I can get kind of aware of it, but I don’t know if I’d call it extremely full.\n\nMaybe uncomfortable sometimes, but I thought that was just because I’m not used to eating much at once. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a significant amount of weight loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it’s been enough that people have noticed. My work pants are pretty loose now, and a couple people have said I look too thin.\n\nBut I don’t know… when I see the number going down, part of me feels like I’m finally doing something right, like I’m getting somewhere. So it’s hard for me to understand why everyone wants me to put it back on. Is that… the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you didn't worry about how your body looks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I probably would. I don’t want to make it sound like it’s only about looks, but when I check the mirror before GED class or softball, my stomach and thighs still look… bigger than they should to me.\n\nAnd then it kind of sets the tone for the whole day, like I’m either doing good or I’m failing. So if I didn’t have that in my head, I think eating more would feel less scary. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would see yourself as overweight even if other people told you that you are underweight?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, probably. I mean, I know people say I’m too thin, and I’m trying to hear that, but when I look in the mirror before class or softball, I still see my stomach and thighs as bigger than they should be.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to argue with everyone, it just feels real to me in the moment. And then it kind of decides whether I feel like I’m doing okay or like I failed for the whole day. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would change if you had a more accurate perception of your body weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably would. I don’t want to overstate it, but a lot of it starts when I look in the mirror, like before class or softball, and my stomach and thighs still look bigger than they should to me.\n\nIf I could see that more clearly, I think eating wouldn’t feel so much like I’m doing something wrong. Right now it kind of makes the whole day feel like I’m either doing good or I’m failing… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight interfere with your daily activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to let it, but it does get in the way a lot. Like if I eat something fried from the break room, or if I have a bigger plate at church, I start worrying it’s going to show on me right away.\n\nThen I keep thinking about it after, and I want to check the scale, like I need to know. I know that probably sounds kind of dramatic, but it can make it hard to just go on with my day normally.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight something that you think about every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is every day, at least in some way. I try not to make it sound like it takes over everything, but it kind of comes up a lot.\n\nLike if I eat fried food from the break room, or a bigger plate at church, I start worrying it’s going to show on me right away. Then I keep thinking about it after, and I get that urge to check the scale, like I need to make sure. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like it’s the only reason, but that fear is a big part of it.\n\nLike if I eat something fried from the break room, or a big plate at church, I start thinking it’s going to show on me right away. Then it’s hard to stop thinking about it, and I want to check the scale just to make sure. So… yeah, I guess that’s mostly what makes me hold back with food. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
